</div></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color: #e4dcf6;line-height:normal;padding:0.2em 0.2em"><div style="height: 4px; width:100%;"></div></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Born</th><td class="infobox-data">25 April 1214<br /><a href="/wiki/Poissy" title="Poissy">Poissy</a>, France</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Died</th><td class="infobox-data">25 August 1270 (aged 56)<br /><a href="/wiki/Tunis" title="Tunis">Tunis</a>, North Africa</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Burial</th><td class="infobox-data"><div style="display:inline" class="label"><a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_St_Denis" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of St Denis">Basilica of St Denis</a></div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Spouse</th><td class="infobox-data"><div style="display:inline;white-space:nowrap;"><div style="display:inline-block;line-height:normal;margin-top:1px;white-space:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Margaret_of_Provence" title="Margaret of Provence">Margaret of Provence</a></div>
   <div style="line-height:0;margin-bottom:-2px;">&#8203;</div>&#32;<div style="display:inline-block;margin-bottom:1px;">&#8203;</div>&#40;<abbr title="married">m.</abbr>&#160;1234&#41;<wbr />&#8203;</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Issue<br /><span style="font-weight:normal"><i><a href="#Children">among others...</a></i></span></th><td class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409"/><div class="plainlist">

</div></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Dynasty" title="Dynasty">House</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/House_of_Capet" title="House of Capet">Capet</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Father</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Louis_VIII,_King_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis VIII, King of France">Louis VIII, King of France</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Mother</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Blanche_of_Castile" title="Blanche of Castile">Blanche of Castile</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Religion</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholicism" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholicism">Roman Catholicism</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Louis IX</b> (25 April 1214&#160;– 25 August 1270), commonly known as <b>Saint Louis</b> or <b>Louis the Saint</b>, was <a href="/wiki/King_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="King of France">King of France</a> from 1226 to 1270, and the most illustrious of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Capet" title="House of Capet">Direct Capetians</a>. He was <a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_the_French_monarch" title="Coronation of the French monarch">crowned in Reims</a> at the age of 12, following the death of his father <a href="/wiki/Louis_VIII" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis VIII">Louis VIII</a>. His mother, <a href="/wiki/Blanche_of_Castile" title="Blanche of Castile">Blanche of Castile</a>, ruled the kingdom as regent until he reached maturity, and then remained his valued adviser until her death. During Louis' childhood, Blanche dealt with the opposition of rebellious vassals and secured Capetian success in the <a href="/wiki/Albigensian_Crusade" title="Albigensian Crusade">Albigensian Crusade</a>, which had started 20 years earlier.
</p><p>As an adult, Louis IX faced recurring conflicts with some of his realm's most powerful nobles, such as <a href="/wiki/Hugh_X_of_Lusignan" title="Hugh X of Lusignan">Hugh X of Lusignan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Peter_of_Dreux" class="mw-redirect" title="Peter of Dreux">Peter of Dreux</a>. Simultaneously, <a href="/wiki/Henry_III_of_England" title="Henry III of England">Henry III of England</a> attempted to restore the <a href="/wiki/Angevin_Empire" title="Angevin Empire">Angevin continental possessions</a>, but was promptly routed at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Taillebourg" title="Battle of Taillebourg">Battle of Taillebourg</a>. Louis annexed several provinces, notably parts of <a href="/wiki/Aquitaine" title="Aquitaine">Aquitaine</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maine_(province)" title="Maine (province)">Maine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Provence" title="Provence">Provence</a>. Honoring a vow he had made while praying for recovery during a serious illness, Louis IX led the ill-fated <a href="/wiki/Seventh_Crusade" title="Seventh Crusade">Seventh Crusade</a> and <a href="/wiki/Eighth_Crusade" title="Eighth Crusade">Eighth Crusade</a> against the Muslim dynasties that ruled North Africa, Egypt and the Holy Land. He was captured and ransomed during the Seventh Crusade, and later died of dysentery during the Eighth Crusade. He was succeeded by his son <a href="/wiki/Philip_III_of_France" title="Philip III of France">Philip III</a>.
</p><p>Louis IX reformed the French legal process, creating a royal justice system in which petitioners could appeal judgements directly to the king. He banned <a href="/wiki/Trial_by_ordeal" title="Trial by ordeal">trials by ordeal</a>, tried to end private wars, and introduced the <a href="/wiki/Presumption_of_innocence" title="Presumption of innocence">presumption of innocence</a> to criminal procedures. To enforce his new legal system, the king created <a href="/wiki/Provost_(military_police)" title="Provost (military police)">provosts</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bailiff_(France)" title="Bailiff (France)">bailiffs</a>. Louis IX enjoyed immense prestige throughout European Christendom. His reign is often remembered as an economic and political golden age for the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_France" title="Kingdom of France">Kingdom of France</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Middle_Ages" title="Middle Ages">Middle Ages</a>. He was largely admired by fellow European rulers and was sometimes asked to arbitrate disputes outside of his kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-goyau_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-goyau-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Louis_IX_&#123;&#123;!&#125;&#125;_king_of_France_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Louis_IX_{{!}}_king_of_France-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His admirers through the centuries have regarded Louis IX as the ideal Christian ruler. His skill as a knight and engaging manner with the public made him popular, though contemporaries occasionally rebuked him as a "monk king".<sup id="cite_ref-Louis_IX_&#123;&#123;!&#125;&#125;_king_of_France_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Louis_IX_{{!}}_king_of_France-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Despite his liberalizing legal reforms, Louis was a devout Christian and enforced strict Catholic orthodoxy. He passed severe laws punishing blasphemy<sup id="cite_ref-Bobineau_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bobineau-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> and targeted France's Jews, including the burning of the <a href="/wiki/Talmud" title="Talmud">Talmud</a> after the <a href="/wiki/Disputation_of_Paris" title="Disputation of Paris">Disputation of Paris</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pope_Who_Saved_the_Talmud_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pope_Who_Saved_the_Talmud-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> He is the only <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> king of France.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sources">Sources</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Sources">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Much of what is known of Louis's life comes from <a href="/wiki/Jean_de_Joinville" title="Jean de Joinville">Jean de Joinville</a>'s famous <i><a href="/wiki/Jean_de_Joinville#Life_of_Saint_Louis" title="Jean de Joinville">Life of Saint Louis</a></i>. Joinville was a close friend, confidant, and counselor to the king. He participated as a witness in the papal inquest into Louis's life that resulted in his canonization in 1297 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Boniface_VIII" title="Pope Boniface VIII">Pope Boniface VIII</a>.
</p><p>Two other important biographies were written by the king's confessor, <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_of_Beaulieu" title="Geoffrey of Beaulieu">Geoffrey of Beaulieu</a>, and his chaplain, <a href="/wiki/William_of_Chartres_(Dominican)" title="William of Chartres (Dominican)">William of Chartres</a>. While several individuals wrote biographies in the decades following the king's death, only Jean of Joinville, Geoffrey of Beaulieu, and William of Chartres wrote from personal knowledge of the king and of the events they describe, and all three are biased favorably to the king. The fourth important source of information is William of Saint-Parthus's 19th-century biography,<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> which he wrote using material from the papal inquest mentioned above.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Louis was born on 25 April 1214 at <a href="/wiki/Poissy" title="Poissy">Poissy</a>, near Paris, the son of <a href="/wiki/Louis_VIII_of_France" title="Louis VIII of France">Louis the Lion</a> and <a href="/wiki/Blanche_of_Castile" title="Blanche of Castile">Blanche of Castile</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> and was baptized there in <i>La Collégiale Notre-Dame</i> church. His grandfather on his father's side was <a href="/wiki/Philip_II_of_France" title="Philip II of France">Philip II</a>, king of France; his grandfather on his mother's side was <a href="/wiki/Alfonso_VIII_of_Castile" title="Alfonso VIII of Castile">Alfonso VIII</a>, king of <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Castile" title="Kingdom of Castile">Castile</a>. <a href="/wiki/Tutors" class="mw-redirect" title="Tutors">Tutors</a> of Blanche's choosing taught him <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a>, public speaking, writing, military arts, and government.<sup id="cite_ref-archstl_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-archstl-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> He was nine years old when his grandfather Philip II died and his father became King Louis VIII.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Louis was 12 years old when his father died on 8 November 1226. He was crowned king within the month at <a href="/wiki/Reims_Cathedral" title="Reims Cathedral">Reims Cathedral</a>. His mother, Blanche, ruled France as <a href="/wiki/Regent" title="Regent">regent</a> during his minority.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta-Louis_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-Louis-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Louis's mother instilled in him her devout Christianity. She is once recorded to have said:<sup id="cite_ref-SHMI_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SHMI-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>I love you, my dear son, as much as a mother can love her child; but I would rather see you dead at my feet than that you should ever commit a mortal sin.</p></blockquote>
<p>His younger brother <a href="/wiki/Charles_I_of_Sicily" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles I of Sicily">Charles I of Sicily</a> (1227–85) was created <a href="/wiki/Count_of_Anjou" class="mw-redirect" title="Count of Anjou">count of Anjou</a>, thus founding the <a href="/wiki/Capetian_House_of_Anjou" title="Capetian House of Anjou">Capetian Angevin</a> dynasty.
</p><p>While his contemporaries viewed his reign as co-rule between the king and his mother, historians generally believe Louis began ruling personally in 1234, with his mother assuming a more advisory role.<sup id="cite_ref-goyau_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-goyau-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> She continued to have a strong influence on the king until her death in 1252.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta-Louis_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-Louis-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEShadis201017-19_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEShadis201017-19-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage">Marriage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Marriage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>On 27 May 1234, Louis married <a href="/wiki/Margaret_of_Provence" title="Margaret of Provence">Margaret of Provence</a> (1221–1295); she was crowned in the cathedral of Sens the next day.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard198364_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard198364-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Margaret was the sister of <a href="/wiki/Eleanor_of_Provence" title="Eleanor of Provence">Eleanor of Provence</a>, who later married <a href="/wiki/Henry_III_of_England" title="Henry III of England">Henry III of England</a>.  The new queen's religious zeal made her a well-suited partner for the king, and they are attested to have gotten along well, enjoying riding together, reading, and listening to music. His closeness to Margaret aroused jealousy in his mother, who tried to keep the couple apart as much as she could.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard198365_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard198365-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Crusades_of_Saint_Louis">The Crusades of Saint Louis</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: The Crusades of Saint Louis">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In 1229, when Louis was 15, his mother ended the <a href="/wiki/Albigensian_Crusade" title="Albigensian Crusade">Albigensian Crusade</a> by signing an agreement with <a href="/wiki/Raymond_VII,_Count_of_Toulouse" title="Raymond VII, Count of Toulouse">Raymond VII of Toulouse</a>. <a href="/wiki/Raymond_VI,_Count_of_Toulouse" title="Raymond VI, Count of Toulouse">Raymond VI of Toulouse</a> had been suspected of ordering the assassination of <a href="/wiki/Pierre_de_Castelnau" title="Pierre de Castelnau">Pierre de Castelnau</a>, a Roman Catholic preacher who attempted to convert the <a href="/wiki/Cathars" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathars">Cathars</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESumption197815_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESumption197815-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Louis went on two crusades: the <a href="/wiki/Seventh_Crusade" title="Seventh Crusade">Seventh Crusade</a> in 1248 and the <a href="/wiki/Eighth_Crusade" title="Eighth Crusade">Eighth Crusade</a> in 1270.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Seventh_Crusade">Seventh Crusade</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Seventh Crusade">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Seventh_Crusade" title="Seventh Crusade">Seventh Crusade</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Gustave_Dor%C3%A9,_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Gustave_Dor%C3%A9%2C_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg/220px-Gustave_Dor%C3%A9%2C_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="283" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Gustave_Dor%C3%A9%2C_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg/330px-Gustave_Dor%C3%A9%2C_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Gustave_Dor%C3%A9%2C_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg/440px-Gustave_Dor%C3%A9%2C_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2395" data-file-height="3077" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Gustave_Dor%C3%A9,_le_d%C3%A9part_de_Louis_IX_pour_la_croisade.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Engraving" title="Engraving">Engraving</a> representing the departure from <a href="/wiki/Aigues-Mortes" title="Aigues-Mortes">Aigues-Mortes</a> of King Louis IX for the <a href="/wiki/Seventh_Crusade" title="Seventh Crusade">Seventh Crusade</a> (by <a href="/wiki/Gustave_Dor%C3%A9" title="Gustave Doré">Gustave Doré</a>)</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Louis_IX,_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre,_Paris_2009.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Statue_of_Louis_IX%2C_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre%2C_Paris_2009.jpg/250px-Statue_of_Louis_IX%2C_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre%2C_Paris_2009.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="166" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Statue_of_Louis_IX%2C_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre%2C_Paris_2009.jpg/375px-Statue_of_Louis_IX%2C_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre%2C_Paris_2009.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Statue_of_Louis_IX%2C_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre%2C_Paris_2009.jpg/500px-Statue_of_Louis_IX%2C_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre%2C_Paris_2009.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3986" data-file-height="2640" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Louis_IX,_Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur_de_Montmartre,_Paris_2009.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Equestrian statue of Saint Louis at the <a href="/wiki/Basilique_du_Sacr%C3%A9-C%C5%93ur,_Paris" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilique du Sacré-Cœur, Paris">Sacré-Cœur</a></div></div></div>
<p>Louis and his followers landed in Egypt on 4 or 5 June 1249 and began their campaign with the capture of the port of <a href="/wiki/Damietta" title="Damietta">Damietta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta-Crusades_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-Crusades-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETyerman2006787_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETyerman2006787-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> This attack caused some disruption in the Muslim <a href="/wiki/Ayyubid_dynasty" title="Ayyubid dynasty">Ayyubid</a> empire, especially as the current sultan, <a href="/wiki/As-Salih_Ayyub" title="As-Salih Ayyub">Al-Malik as-Salih Najm al-Din Ayyub</a>, was on his deathbed. However, the march of Europeans from Damietta toward <a href="/wiki/Cairo" title="Cairo">Cairo</a> through the <a href="/wiki/Nile_River_Delta" class="mw-redirect" title="Nile River Delta">Nile River Delta</a> went slowly. The seasonal rising of the Nile and the summer heat made it impossible for them to advance.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> During this time, the Ayyubid sultan died, and the sultan's wife <a href="/wiki/Shajar_al-Durr" title="Shajar al-Durr">Shajar al-Durr</a> set in motion a shift in power that would make her Queen and eventually result in the rule of the Egyptian army of the <a href="/wiki/Mamluks" class="mw-redirect" title="Mamluks">Mamluks</a>.
</p><p>On 8 February 1250, Louis lost his army at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Mansurah_(1250)" title="Battle of Mansurah (1250)">Battle of Al Mansurah</a><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDupuy1993417_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDupuy1993417-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> and was captured by the Egyptians. His release was eventually negotiated in return for a ransom of 400,000 <i><a href="/wiki/Livre_tournois" title="Livre tournois">livres tournois</a></i> (roughly 80 million USD today) and the surrender of the city of Damietta.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETyerman2006796_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETyerman2006796-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:152px;"><a href="/wiki/File:C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg/150px-C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="194" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg/225px-C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg/300px-C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2183" data-file-height="2827" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:C_croisade7_prisonnier1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Louis IX was taken prisoner at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Fariskur_(1250)" title="Battle of Fariskur (1250)">Battle of Fariskur</a>, during the Seventh Crusade (<a href="/wiki/Gustave_Dor%C3%A9" title="Gustave Doré">Gustave Doré</a>).</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Four_years_in_the_Kingdom_of_Jerusalem">Four years in the Kingdom of Jerusalem</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Four years in the Kingdom of Jerusalem">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Following his release from Egyptian captivity, Louis spent four years in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Jerusalem" title="Kingdom of Jerusalem">Kingdom of Jerusalem</a>, namely in <a href="/wiki/Acre,_Israel" title="Acre, Israel">Acre</a>, <a href="/wiki/Caesarea" title="Caesarea">Caesarea</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jaffa" title="Jaffa">Jaffa</a>. He used his wealth to assist the <a href="/wiki/Crusaders" class="mw-redirect" title="Crusaders">Crusaders</a> in rebuilding their defenses<sup id="cite_ref-brehier_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brehier-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> and conducted diplomacy with the Islamic powers of <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a> and Egypt. In the spring of 1254, he and his surviving army returned to France.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta-Crusades_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-Crusades-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Louis exchanged multiple letters and emissaries with <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Empire" title="Mongol Empire">Mongol</a> rulers of the period. During his first crusade in 1248, Louis was approached by envoys from <a href="/wiki/Eljigidei" title="Eljigidei">Eljigidei</a>, the Mongol military commander stationed in <a href="/wiki/Mongol_Armenia" title="Mongol Armenia">Armenia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ilkhanate" title="Ilkhanate">Persia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJackson1980481-513_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJackson1980481-513-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Eljigidei suggested that King Louis should land in Egypt while Eljigidei attacked <a href="/wiki/Baghdad" title="Baghdad">Baghdad</a>, in order to prevent the Muslims of Egypt and Syria from joining forces. Louis sent <a href="/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_de_Longjumeau" title="André de Longjumeau">André de Longjumeau</a>, a Dominican priest, as an emissary to the Great Khan <a href="/wiki/G%C3%BCy%C3%BCk" class="mw-redirect" title="Güyük">Güyük Khan</a> (r. 1246–48) in Mongolia. Güyük died before the emissary arrived at his court, however, and Güyük's queen and now regent, <a href="/wiki/Oghul_Qaimish" title="Oghul Qaimish">Oghul Qaimish</a>, turned down the diplomatic offer.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Louis dispatched another envoy to the Mongol court, the Franciscan <a href="/wiki/William_of_Rubruck" title="William of Rubruck">William of Rubruck</a>, who visited the Great Khan <a href="/wiki/M%C3%B6ngke" class="mw-redirect" title="Möngke">Möngke</a> (1251–1259) in Mongolia. He spent several years at the Mongol court. In 1259, <a href="/wiki/Berke" title="Berke">Berke</a>, the ruler of the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Horde" title="Golden Horde">Golden Horde</a>, demanded the submission of Louis.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> By contrast, Mongolian emperors Möngke and <a href="/wiki/Kublai_Khan" title="Kublai Khan">Khubilai</a>'s brother, the <a href="/wiki/Ilkhan" class="mw-redirect" title="Ilkhan">Ilkhan</a> Hulegu, sent a letter to the king of France seeking his military assistance, but the letter never reached France.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Eighth_Crusade">Eighth Crusade</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Eighth Crusade">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg/220px-Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="243" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg/330px-Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg/440px-Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1046" data-file-height="1154" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mort_de_Louis_IX_le_Saint.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Death of Saint Louis: On 25 August 1270, Saint Louis dies in his tent, ornamented with royal symbols, near <a href="/wiki/Tunis" title="Tunis">Tunis</a>. Illuminated by <a href="/wiki/Jean_Fouquet" title="Jean Fouquet">Jean Fouquet</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Grandes_Chroniques_de_France" title="Grandes Chroniques de France">Grandes Chroniques de France</a></i> (1455–1460)</div></div></div>
<p>In a parliament held at Paris, 24 March 1267, Louis and his three sons "took the cross." On hearing the reports of the missionaries, Louis resolved to land at <a href="/wiki/Tunis" title="Tunis">Tunis</a>, and he ordered his younger brother, Charles of Anjou, to join him there. The crusaders, among whom was the English prince <a href="/wiki/Edward_Longshanks" class="mw-redirect" title="Edward Longshanks">Edward Longshanks</a>, landed at <a href="/wiki/Carthage" title="Carthage">Carthage</a> 17 July 1270, but disease broke out in the camp. Many died of <a href="/wiki/Dysentery" title="Dysentery">dysentery</a>, and on 25 August, Louis himself died.<sup id="cite_ref-brehier_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brehier-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-MagillAves606_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MagillAves606-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Patron_of_arts_and_arbiter_of_Europe">Patron of arts and arbiter of Europe</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Patron of arts and arbiter of Europe">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Louis's patronage of the arts inspired much innovation in <a href="/wiki/Gothic_art" title="Gothic art">Gothic art</a> and <a href="/wiki/Gothic_architecture" title="Gothic architecture">architecture</a>. The style of his court was influential throughout Europe, both because of artwork purchased from Parisian masters for export, and by the marriage of the king's daughters and other female relatives to foreigners. They became emissaries of Parisian models and styles elsewhere. Louis's personal chapel, the <i><a href="/wiki/Sainte-Chapelle" title="Sainte-Chapelle">Sainte-Chapelle</a></i> in Paris, which was known for its intricate stained-glass windows, was copied more than once by his descendants elsewhere. Louis is believed to have ordered the production of the <a href="/wiki/Morgan_Bible" title="Morgan Bible">Morgan Bible</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Arsenal_Bible" class="mw-redirect" title="Arsenal Bible">Arsenal Bible</a>, both deluxe <a href="/wiki/Illuminated_manuscript" title="Illuminated manuscript">illuminated manuscripts</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Louis-innocentiv.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/df/Louis-innocentiv.jpg/220px-Louis-innocentiv.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="270" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/d/df/Louis-innocentiv.jpg/330px-Louis-innocentiv.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/d/df/Louis-innocentiv.jpg 2x" data-file-width="344" data-file-height="422" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Louis-innocentiv.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_IV" title="Pope Innocent IV">Pope Innocent IV</a> with Louis IX at <a href="/wiki/Cluny" title="Cluny">Cluny</a></div></div></div>
<p>During the so-called "golden century of Saint Louis", the kingdom of France was at its height in Europe, both politically and economically. Saint Louis was regarded as <i>"<a href="/wiki/Primus_inter_pares" title="Primus inter pares">primus inter pares</a>",</i> first among equals, among the kings and rulers of the continent. He commanded the largest army and ruled the largest and wealthiest kingdom, the European centre of arts and intellectual thought at the time. The foundations for the notable college of theology, later known as the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Paris" title="University of Paris">Sorbonne</a>, were laid in Paris about the year 1257.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The prestige and respect felt by Europeans for King Louis IX were due more to the appeal of his personality than to military domination. For his contemporaries, he was the quintessential example of the Christian prince and embodied the whole of <a href="/wiki/Christendom" title="Christendom">Christendom</a> in his person. His reputation for fairness and even saintliness was already well established while he was alive, and on many occasions he was chosen as an arbiter in quarrels among the rulers of Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-goyau_1-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-goyau-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Shortly before 1256, <a href="/wiki/Enguerrand_IV,_Lord_of_Coucy" class="mw-redirect" title="Enguerrand IV, Lord of Coucy">Enguerrand IV, Lord of Coucy</a>, arrested and without trial hanged three young squires of Laon, whom he accused of poaching in his forest. In 1256 Louis had the lord arrested and brought to the <a href="/wiki/Palais_du_Louvre" class="mw-redirect" title="Palais du Louvre">Louvre</a> by his sergeants. Enguerrand demanded judgment by his peers and trial by battle, which the king refused because he thought it obsolete. Enguerrand was tried, sentenced, and ordered to pay 12,000 livres. Part of the money was to pay for <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">masses</a> to be said in perpetuity for the souls of the men he had hanged.
</p><p>In 1258, Louis and <a href="/wiki/James_I_of_Aragon" title="James I of Aragon">James I of Aragon</a> signed the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Corbeil_(1258)" title="Treaty of Corbeil (1258)">Treaty of Corbeil</a> to end areas of contention between them. By this treaty, Louis renounced his feudal overlordship over the <a href="/wiki/County_of_Barcelona" title="County of Barcelona">County of Barcelona</a> and <a href="/wiki/Roussillon" title="Roussillon">Roussillon</a>, which was held by the King of Aragon. James in turn renounced his feudal overlordship over several counties in southern France, including <a href="/wiki/Provence" title="Provence">Provence</a> and <a href="/wiki/Languedoc" title="Languedoc">Languedoc</a>. In 1259 Louis signed the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1259)" title="Treaty of Paris (1259)">Treaty of Paris</a>, by which <a href="/wiki/Henry_III_of_England" title="Henry III of England">Henry III of England</a> was confirmed in his possession of territories in southwestern France, and Louis received the provinces of <a href="/wiki/County_of_Anjou" title="County of Anjou">Anjou</a>, <a href="/wiki/Normandy" title="Normandy">Normandy</a> (Normandie), <a href="/wiki/Poitou" title="Poitou">Poitou</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maine_(province)" title="Maine (province)">Maine</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Touraine" title="Touraine">Touraine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Encarta-Louis_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encarta-Louis-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Religious_nature">Religious nature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Religious nature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg/220px-SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="205" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg/330px-SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg/440px-SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1194" data-file-height="1110" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SaintLouisTuniqueAndDiscipline.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Cilice" title="Cilice">Hair shirt</a> and <a href="/wiki/Scourge" title="Scourge">scourge</a> of Louis IX. Treasury of <a href="/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Paris" title="Notre-Dame de Paris">Notre-Dame de Paris</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The perception of Louis IX by his contemporaries as the exemplary Christian prince was reinforced by his religious zeal. Louis was an extremely devout Catholic, and he built the <i><a href="/wiki/Sainte-Chapelle" title="Sainte-Chapelle">Sainte-Chapelle</a></i> ("Holy Chapel"),<sup id="cite_ref-goyau_1-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-goyau-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> located within the royal palace complex (now the <a href="/wiki/Paris_Hall_of_Justice" class="mw-redirect" title="Paris Hall of Justice">Paris Hall of Justice</a>), on the <i><a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_de_la_Cit%C3%A9" title="Île de la Cité">Île de la Cité</a></i> in the centre of Paris. The <i>Sainte Chapelle</i>, a prime example of the <a href="/wiki/Rayonnant" title="Rayonnant">Rayonnant</a> style of <a href="/wiki/Gothic_architecture" title="Gothic architecture">Gothic architecture</a>, was erected as a shrine for what Louis believed to be the <a href="/wiki/Crown_of_Thorns" class="mw-redirect" title="Crown of Thorns">Crown of Thorns</a> and a fragment of the <a href="/wiki/True_Cross" title="True Cross">True Cross</a>, supposed precious <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">relics</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Passion_(Christianity)" class="mw-redirect" title="Passion (Christianity)">Passion</a> of Christ. He acquired these in 1239–41 from Emperor <a href="/wiki/Baldwin_II,_Latin_Emperor" title="Baldwin II, Latin Emperor">Baldwin II</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Latin_Empire" title="Latin Empire">Latin Empire</a> of <a href="/wiki/Constantinople" title="Constantinople">Constantinople</a> by agreeing to pay off Baldwin's debt to the Venetian merchant Niccolo Quirino, for which Baldwin had pledged the Crown of Thorns as collateral.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Louis IX paid the exorbitant sum of 135,000 <a href="/wiki/French_livre" title="French livre">livres</a> to clear the debt.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:San_Domenico47.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/San_Domenico47.jpg/220px-San_Domenico47.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="225" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/San_Domenico47.jpg/330px-San_Domenico47.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/San_Domenico47.jpg/440px-San_Domenico47.jpg 2x" data-file-width="898" data-file-height="920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:San_Domenico47.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Reliquary" title="Reliquary">Reliquary</a> of Saint Louis (end of the 13th century) <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Dominic" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of Saint Dominic">Basilica of Saint Dominic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bologna" title="Bologna">Bologna</a>, Italy</div></div></div>
<p>In 1230, the King forbade all forms of <a href="/wiki/Usury" title="Usury">usury</a>, defined at the time as any taking of interest and therefore covering most banking activities. Louis used these anti-usury laws to extract funds from Jewish and Lombard moneylenders, with the hopes that it would help pay for a future crusade.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_18-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> Louis also oversaw the <a href="/wiki/Disputation_of_Paris" title="Disputation of Paris">Disputation of Paris</a> in 1240, in which Paris's Jewish leaders were imprisoned and forced to admit to anti-Christian passages in the Talmud, the major source of Jewish commentaries on the Bible and religious law. As a result of the disputation, Pope <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gregory_IX" title="Pope Gregory IX">Gregory IX</a> declared that all copies of the Talmud should be seized and destroyed. In 1243, Louis ordered the burning of 12,000 Talmudim, along with other important Jewish books and scripture.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The edict against the Talmud was eventually overturned by Gregory IX's successor, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_IV" title="Pope Innocent IV">Innocent IV</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Pope_Who_Saved_the_Talmud_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Pope_Who_Saved_the_Talmud-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Louis also expanded the scope of the <a href="/wiki/Inquisition" title="Inquisition">Inquisition</a> in France. He set the punishment for <a href="/wiki/Blasphemy" title="Blasphemy">blasphemy</a> to <a href="/wiki/Mutilation" title="Mutilation">mutilation</a> of the tongue and lips.<sup id="cite_ref-Bobineau_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bobineau-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The area most affected by this expansion was southern France, where the <a href="/wiki/Cathar" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathar">Cathar</a> sect had been strongest. The rate of confiscation of property from the Cathars and others reached its highest levels in the years before his first crusade and slowed upon his return to France in 1254.
</p><p>In 1250, Louis headed a crusade to Egypt and was taken prisoner. During his captivity, he recited the <a href="/wiki/Canonical_hours" title="Canonical hours">Divine Office</a> every day. After his release against ransom, he visited the Holy Land before returning to France.<sup id="cite_ref-SHMI_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SHMI-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> In these deeds, Louis IX tried to fulfill what he considered the duty of France as "the eldest daughter of the Church" (<i>la fille aînée de l'Église</i>), a tradition of protector of the Church going back to the <a href="/wiki/Franks" title="Franks">Franks</a> and <a href="/wiki/Charlemagne" title="Charlemagne">Charlemagne</a>, who had been crowned by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Leo_III" title="Pope Leo III">Pope Leo III</a> in <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> in 800. The kings of France were known in the Church by the title "most Christian king" (<i>Rex Christianissimus</i>).
</p><p>Louis founded many hospitals and houses: the House of the <a href="/wiki/Filles-Dieu" title="Filles-Dieu">Filles-Dieu</a> for reformed prostitutes; the <a href="/wiki/Quinze-Vingts_National_Eye_Hospital" class="mw-redirect" title="Quinze-Vingts National Eye Hospital">Quinze-Vingt</a> for 300 blind men (1254), and hospitals at Pontoise, Vernon, and Compiégne.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>St. Louis installed a house of the <a href="/wiki/Trinitarian_Order" class="mw-redirect" title="Trinitarian Order">Trinitarian Order</a> at <a href="/wiki/Palace_of_Fontainebleau" title="Palace of Fontainebleau">Fontainebleau</a>, his chateau and estate near Paris. He chose Trinitarians as his chaplains and was accompanied by them on his crusades. In his spiritual testament he wrote, "My dearest son, you should permit yourself to be tormented by every kind of martyrdom before you would allow yourself to commit a mortal sin."<sup id="cite_ref-SHMI_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SHMI-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:left;"></div>
<p>Louis authored and sent the <i>Enseignements</i>, or teachings, to his son <a href="/wiki/Philip_III_of_France" title="Philip III of France">Philip III</a>. The letter outlined how Philip should follow the example of Jesus Christ in order to be a moral leader.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The letter is estimated to have been written in 1267, three years before Louis's death.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Children">Children</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Children">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ol><li>Blanche (12 July/4 December 1240 – 29 April 1244), died in infancy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Isabella_of_France,_Queen_of_Navarre" title="Isabella of France, Queen of Navarre">Isabella</a> (2 March 1241 – 28 January 1271), married <a href="/wiki/Theobald_II_of_Navarre" title="Theobald II of Navarre">Theobald II of Navarre</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJordan201725_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJordan201725-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Louis_of_France_(1244%E2%80%931260)" title="Louis of France (1244–1260)">Louis</a> (23 September 1243/24 February 1244 – 11 January/2 February 1260). Betrothed to <a href="/wiki/Berengaria_of_Castile,_Lady_of_Guadalajara" title="Berengaria of Castile, Lady of Guadalajara">Berengaria of Castile</a> in Paris on 20 August 1255.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJordan201725-26_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJordan201725-26-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Philip_III_of_France" title="Philip III of France">Philip III</a> (1 May 1245 – 5 October 1285), married firstly to <a href="/wiki/Isabella_of_Aragon,_Queen_of_France" title="Isabella of Aragon, Queen of France">Isabella of Aragon</a> in 1262 and secondly to <a href="/wiki/Marie_of_Brabant,_Queen_of_France" title="Marie of Brabant, Queen of France">Maria of Brabant</a> in 1274.</li>
<li>John (1246/1247 – 10 March 1248), died in infancy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Tristan,_Count_of_Valois" title="John Tristan, Count of Valois">John Tristan</a> (8 April 1250 – 3 August 1270), Count of Valois, married <a href="/wiki/Yolande_II,_Countess_of_Nevers" title="Yolande II, Countess of Nevers">Yolande II, Countess of Nevers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Peter_I,_Count_of_Alen%C3%A7on" title="Peter I, Count of Alençon">Peter</a> (1251 – 6/7 April 1284),<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Count of Perche and Alençon, married <a href="/wiki/Joanne_of_Ch%C3%A2tillon" class="mw-redirect" title="Joanne of Châtillon">Joanne of Châtillon</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Blanche_of_France,_Infanta_of_Castile" title="Blanche of France, Infanta of Castile">Blanche</a> (early 1253 – 17 June 1320), married <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_de_la_Cerda,_Infante_of_Castile" class="mw-redirect" title="Ferdinand de la Cerda, Infante of Castile">Ferdinand de la Cerda, Infante of Castile</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Margaret_of_France,_Duchess_of_Brabant" title="Margaret of France, Duchess of Brabant">Margaret</a> (early 1255 – July 1271), married <a href="/wiki/John_I,_Duke_of_Brabant" title="John I, Duke of Brabant">John I, Duke of Brabant</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Robert,_Count_of_Clermont" title="Robert, Count of Clermont">Robert</a> (1256 – 7 February 1317), Count of Clermont,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> married <a href="/wiki/Beatrice_of_Burgundy,_Lady_of_Bourbon" title="Beatrice of Burgundy, Lady of Bourbon">Beatrice of Burgundy</a>. The French crown devolved upon his male-line descendant, <a href="/wiki/Henry_IV_of_France" title="Henry IV of France">Henry IV</a> (the first <a href="/wiki/House_of_Bourbon" title="House of Bourbon">Bourbon</a> king), when the legitimate male line of Philip III died out in 1589.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Agnes_of_France,_Duchess_of_Burgundy" title="Agnes of France, Duchess of Burgundy">Agnes</a> (1260 – 19/20 December 1327), married <a href="/wiki/Robert_II,_Duke_of_Burgundy" title="Robert II, Duke of Burgundy">Robert II, Duke of Burgundy</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv_7-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERichard1983xxiv-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup></li></ol>
<p>Louis and Margaret's two children who died in infancy were first buried at the <a href="/wiki/Cistercians" title="Cistercians">Cistercian</a> <a href="/wiki/Royaumont_Abbey" title="Royaumont Abbey">abbey of Royaumont</a>. In 1820 they were transferred and reinterred to <a href="/wiki/Saint-Denis_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint-Denis Basilica">Saint-Denis Basilica</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBrown1990810_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrown1990810-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death_and_legacy">Death and legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Death and legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_MS_19_D_I,_fol._227r.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/3f/Royal_MS_19_D_I%2C_fol._227r.png/220px-Royal_MS_19_D_I%2C_fol._227r.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="166" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/3f/Royal_MS_19_D_I%2C_fol._227r.png/330px-Royal_MS_19_D_I%2C_fol._227r.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/3f/Royal_MS_19_D_I%2C_fol._227r.png/440px-Royal_MS_19_D_I%2C_fol._227r.png 2x" data-file-width="937" data-file-height="708" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_MS_19_D_I,_fol._227r.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Louis's body returning, from a copy of the crusade treatise <i><a href="/wiki/Directorium_ad_passagium" class="mw-redirect" title="Directorium ad passagium">Directorium ad passagium</a></i></div></div></div>
<p>Louis died at <a href="/wiki/Tunis" title="Tunis">Tunis</a> on 25 August 1270, in an epidemic of <a href="/wiki/Dysentery" title="Dysentery">dysentery</a> that swept through his army.<sup id="cite_ref-MagillAves606_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MagillAves606-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> According to European custom, his body was subjected to the process known as <i><a href="/wiki/Mos_Teutonicus" title="Mos Teutonicus">mos Teutonicus</a></i> prior to his remains being returned to France.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWesterhof200879_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWesterhof200879-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> Louis was succeeded as King of France by his son, <a href="/wiki/Philip_III_of_France" title="Philip III of France">Philip III</a>.
</p><p>Louis's younger brother, <a href="/wiki/Charles_I_of_Naples" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles I of Naples">Charles I of Naples</a>, preserved his heart and intestines, and conveyed them for burial in the cathedral of <a href="/wiki/Monreale" title="Monreale">Monreale</a> near <a href="/wiki/Palermo" title="Palermo">Palermo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Louis's bones were carried overland in a lengthy processional across Sicily, Italy, the Alps, and France, until they were interred in the royal necropolis at <a href="/wiki/Saint_Denis_Basilica" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Denis Basilica">Saint-Denis</a> in May 1271.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Charles and Philip III later dispersed a number of <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">relics</a> to promote Louis's veneration.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancestry">Ancestry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Ancestry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration_as_a_saint">Veneration as a saint</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Veneration as a saint">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above n" style="background-color:gold;"><div class="honorific-prefix" style="display:inline;font-size: 77%; font-weight: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">Saint</a></div><br /><div style="display:inline;" class="fn">Louis</div></th></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:San_Luis,_rey_de_Francia,_de_Francisco_Pacheco_(Museo_de_Bellas_Artes_de_Sevilla).jpg" class="image"><img King of France) by <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Pacheco" title="Francisco Pacheco">Francisco Pacheco</a></div></td></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color:gold;">King of France <br /> <a href="/wiki/Confessor" title="Confessor">Confessor</a></th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Venerated&#160;in</th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican Communion</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonized</a></th><td class="infobox-data">11 July 1297, <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a>, <a href="/wiki/Papal_States" title="Papal States">Papal States</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Boniface_VIII" title="Pope Boniface VIII">Pope Boniface VIII</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data">25 August</td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data">The <a href="/wiki/Crown_of_Thorns" class="mw-redirect" title="Crown of Thorns">Crown of Thorns</a>, <a href="/wiki/Crown" title="Crown">crown</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sceptre" title="Sceptre">sceptre</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Globus_cruciger" title="Globus cruciger">globus cruciger</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Sword" title="Sword">sword</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Fleur-de-lis" title="Fleur-de-lis">fleur-de-lis</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Mantle_(royal_garment)" title="Mantle (royal garment)">mantle</a>, and the other parts of the <a href="/wiki/French_regalia" class="mw-redirect" title="French regalia">French regalia</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409"/><div class="plainlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Third_Order_of_Saint_Francis" title="Third Order of Saint Francis">Third Order of Saint Francis</a></li><li>The <a href="/wiki/French_Monarchy" class="mw-redirect" title="French Monarchy">French Monarchy</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_New_Orleans" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of New Orleans">Archdiocese of New Orleans</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Port-Louis" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Port-Louis">Diocese of Port-Louis</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Barber" title="Barber">barbers</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/Groom" class="mw-redirect" title="Groom">grooms</a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><a href="/wiki/Pope_Boniface_VIII" title="Pope Boniface VIII">Pope Boniface VIII</a> proclaimed the canonization of Louis in 1297;<sup id="cite_ref-ODS_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ODS-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> he is the only French king to be declared a <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Louis IX is often considered the model of the ideal Christian monarch.<sup id="cite_ref-ODS_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ODS-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Named in his honour, the <a href="/wiki/Sisters_of_Charity_of_St._Louis" title="Sisters of Charity of St. Louis">Sisters of Charity of St. Louis</a> is a Roman Catholic <a href="/wiki/Religious_order_(Catholic)" title="Religious order (Catholic)">religious order</a> founded in <a href="/wiki/Vannes" title="Vannes">Vannes</a>, France, in 1803.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> A similar order, the Sisters of St Louis, was founded in <a href="/wiki/Juilly,_Seine-et-Marne" title="Juilly, Seine-et-Marne">Juilly</a> in 1842.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He is honoured as co-patron of the <a href="/wiki/Third_Order_of_St._Francis" class="mw-redirect" title="Third Order of St. Francis">Third Order of St. Francis</a>, which claims him as a member of the Order. When he became king, over a hundred poor people were served meals in his house on ordinary days. Often the king served these guests himself. His acts of charity, coupled with his devout religious practices, gave rise to the legend that he joined the Third Order of St. Francis, though it is unlikely that he ever actually joined the order.<sup id="cite_ref-archstl_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-archstl-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Catholic Church and <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church)" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church)">Episcopal Church</a> honor him with a feast day on August 25.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Things_named_after_Saint_Louis">Things named after Saint Louis</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Things named after Saint Louis">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>The French royal <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Saint_Louis" title="Order of Saint Louis">Order of Saint Louis</a> (1693–1790 and 1814–1830)<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Places">Places</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Places">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Many countries in which French speakers and Catholicism were prevalent named places after King Louis:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/San_Luis_Province" title="San Luis Province">San Luis Province</a> in Argentina;<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/San_Luis_Potos%C3%AD" title="San Luis Potosí">San Luis Potosí</a> in Mexico;<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Multiple locations in the United States
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/St._Louis" title="St. Louis">St. Louis, Missouri</a>, named by French colonists<sup id="cite_ref-CDWPN_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CDWPN-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></li>
<li>Multiple locations in France<sup id="cite_ref-CDWPN_51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CDWPN-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/%C3%8Ele_Saint-Louis" title="Île Saint-Louis">Île Saint-Louis</a>, an island in the river <a href="/wiki/Seine" title="Seine">Seine</a>, Paris, France<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Saint-Louis,_New_Caledonia" title="Saint-Louis, New Caledonia">Saint-Louis, New Caledonia</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li></ul></li>
<li>Multiple locations in Canada<sup id="cite_ref-CDWPN_51-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CDWPN-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Saint-Louis,_Senegal" title="Saint-Louis, Senegal">Saint-Louis, Senegal</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-CDWPN_51-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CDWPN-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Lu%C3%ADs,_Maranh%C3%A3o" title="São Luís, Maranhão">São Luís, Maranhão</a> in Brazil<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The Philippines
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/San_Luis,_Aurora" title="San Luis, Aurora">San Luis, Aurora</a> in the Philippines<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/San_Luis,_Pampanga" title="San Luis, Pampanga">San Luis, Pampanga</a> in the Philippines<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/San_Luis,_Batangas" title="San Luis, Batangas">San Luis, Batangas</a> in the Philippines<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li></ul></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Buildings">Buildings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Buildings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>France
<ul><li>A hospital in the 10th arrondissement of Paris<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Versailles_Cathedral" title="Versailles Cathedral">Cathédrale Saint-Louis de Versailles</a> in <a href="/wiki/Versailles,_Yvelines" title="Versailles, Yvelines">Versailles</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li></ul></li>
<li>United States
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_St._Louis,_King_of_France" title="Basilica of St. Louis, King of France">Basilica of St. Louis, King of France</a>,  completed in 1834 in St. Louis, Missouri<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_Basilica_of_Saint_Louis_(St._Louis)" title="Cathedral Basilica of Saint Louis (St. Louis)">Cathedral Basilica of St. Louis</a>,  completed in 1914 in St. Louis, Missouri <sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/St._Louis_Cathedral_(New_Orleans)" title="St. Louis Cathedral (New Orleans)">St. Louis Cathedral in New Orleans</a><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Saint Louis Catholic High School, in Lake Charles, Louisiana</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mission_San_Luis_Rey_de_Francia" title="Mission San Luis Rey de Francia">Mission San Luis Rey de Francia</a>, Oceanside, California, founded June 12, 1798</li></ul></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/San_Luigi_dei_Francesi" title="San Luigi dei Francesi">national church of France</a> in Rome: San Luigi dei Francesi in Italian, or Saint Louis of France in English.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_St_Louis_(Plovdiv)" title="Cathedral of St Louis (Plovdiv)">Cathedral of St Louis</a> in Plovdiv, Bulgaria<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Acropolium_of_Carthage" title="Acropolium of Carthage">Cathedral of St Louis</a> in Carthage, Tunisia, so named because Louis IX died at that approximate location in 1270<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/List_of_churches_in_Moscow#Centre" title="List of churches in Moscow">Church of St Louis</a> in Moscow, Russia<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Rue Saint Louis of <a href="/wiki/Pondicherry_(city)" class="mw-redirect" title="Pondicherry (city)">Pondicherry</a>, India.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The Convent of Saint Louis and Catholic High School in Carrickmacross, Ireland.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_portraits">Notable portraits</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Notable portraits">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>United States
<ul><li>A bas-relief of St. Louis is one of the carved portraits of historic lawmakers that adorn <a href="/wiki/United_States_Capitol#House_Chamber" title="United States Capitol">the chamber</a> of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives" title="United States House of Representatives">United States House of Representatives</a>.</li>
<li>Saint Louis is also portrayed on a <a href="/wiki/Frieze" title="Frieze">frieze</a> depicting a timeline of important lawgivers throughout world history, on the North Wall of the <a href="/wiki/Courtroom" title="Courtroom">Courtroom</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_the_United_States" title="Supreme Court of the United States">Supreme Court of the United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>A statue of St. Louis by the sculptor John Donoghue stands on the roofline of the <a href="/wiki/New_York_State_Appellate_Division" class="mw-redirect" title="New York State Appellate Division">New York State Appellate Division</a> Court at 27 Madison Avenue in New York City.</li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Apotheosis_of_St._Louis" title="Apotheosis of St. Louis">Apotheosis of St. Louis</a> is an equestrian statue of the saint, by <a href="/wiki/Charles_Henry_Niehaus" title="Charles Henry Niehaus">Charles Henry Niehaus</a>, that stands in front of the <a href="/wiki/Saint_Louis_Art_Museum" title="Saint Louis Art Museum">Saint Louis Art Museum</a> in Forest Park.</li>
<li>A heroic portrait by <a href="/wiki/Charles_de_Steuben" title="Charles de Steuben">Baron Charles de Steuben</a> hangs in the <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_the_National_Shrine_of_the_Assumption_of_the_Blessed_Virgin_Mary_(Baltimore)" class="mw-redirect" title="Basilica of the National Shrine of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary (Baltimore)">Basilica of the National Shrine of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary</a> in <a href="/wiki/Baltimore" title="Baltimore">Baltimore</a>.  An 1821 gift of <a href="/wiki/Louis_XVIII_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis XVIII of France">King Louis XVIII of France</a>, it depicts St. Louis burying his plague-stricken troops before the siege of <a href="/wiki/Tunis" title="Tunis">Tunis</a> at the beginning of the <a href="/wiki/Eighth_Crusade" title="Eighth Crusade">Eighth Crusade</a> in 1270.</li></ul></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_fiction">In fiction</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: In fiction">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>Davis, William Stearns, <i>"Falaise of the Blessed Voice"</i> aka <i>"The White Queen"</i>. New York, NY: Macmillan, 1904</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Peter_Berling" title="Peter Berling">Peter Berling</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Children_of_the_Grail" title="The Children of the Grail">The Children of the Grail</a></i></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Jules_Verne" title="Jules Verne">Jules Verne</a>, "To the Sun?/Off on a Comet!" A comet takes several bits of the Earth away when it grazes the Earth. Some people, taken up at the same time, find the Tomb of Saint Louis is one of the bits, as they explore the comet.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Adam_Gidwitz" title="Adam Gidwitz">Adam Gidwitz</a>, <i>The Inquisitor's Tale</i></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dante_Alighieri" title="Dante Alighieri">Dante Alighieri</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Divine_Comedy" title="Divine Comedy">Divina Commedia</a>.</i> It is likely that Dante hides the figure of the Saint King behind the Veltro, the Messo di Dio, the Veglio di Creta and the "515", which is a duplicate of the Messo. This is a trinitarian representation to oppose to the analogous representation of his nephew <a href="/wiki/Philip_IV_of_France" title="Philip IV of France">Philip IV the Fair</a>, as the <a href="/wiki/The_Beast_(Revelation)" title="The Beast (Revelation)">Beast from the Sea</a>. The idea came to Dante from the transposition of the Revelation of St. John in the history, studied from the abbot and theologian <a href="/wiki/Joachim_of_Fiore" title="Joachim of Fiore">Joachim of Fiore</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Louis_IX_of_France&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Arnaud_du_Prat&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Arnaud du Prat (page does not exist)">Arnaud du Prat</a>, Paris canon; Rhymed, chanted office for St. Louis, 1290, Sens Bib. Mun. MS6, and elsewhere.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marc-Antoine_Charpentier" title="Marc-Antoine Charpentier">Marc-Antoine Charpentier</a>, <i>Motet for Saint Louis,</i> H.320, for 1 voice, 2 treble instruments (?) and continuo 1675.</li>
<li>Marc-Antoine Charpentier<i>,</i> Motet <i>In honorem santi Ludovici Regis Galliae canticum tribus vocibus cum symphonia,</i> H.323, for 3 voices, 2 treble instruments and continuo (1678&#160;?)</li>
<li>Marc-Antoine Charpentier<i>,</i> Motet <i>In honorem Sancti Ludovici regis Galliae,</i> H.332, for 3 voices, 2 treble instruments and continuo 1683)</li>
<li>Marc-Antoine Charpentier, Motet <i>In honorem Sancti Ludovici regis Galliae canticum,</i> H.365 &amp; H.365 a, for soloists, chorus, woodwinds, strings and continuo (1690)</li>
<li>Marc-Antoine Charpentier, Motet <i>In honorem Sancti Ludovici regis Galliae,</i> H.418, for soloists, chorus, 2 flutes, 2 violins and continuo (1692–93)</li></ul>
